<a id="going-abroad-from-bangladesh-faq-dalal-scams-departure-bottlenecks-and-illegal-routes"></a>

# Going abroad from Bangladesh: FAQ, dalal scams, departure bottlenecks and illegal routes

Sources checked up to 22 Nov 2026.

What people in Bangladesh who want to work abroad need to know across every country, as of November 2026: the questions they ask most, the tricks brokers use, where they get stuck or asked for bribes when leaving and returning, and how the irregular routes end. Each country's own doc, in the `countries` folder, gives the full steps for that country. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO (non-governmental organisation), research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed.

<a id="contents"></a>

<a id="frequently-asked-questions"></a>

## 1 Frequently asked questions

<a id="deciding"></a>

### 1.1 Deciding

<a id="which-countries-can-i-go-to-for-work-right-now"></a>

#### 1.1.1 Which countries can I go to for work right now?

As of November 2026:
- **Saudi Arabia:** open, and the largest destination, though departures from January to 17 October 2026 were 45% below the same period of 2025 <sup>[1](#o1), [N1](#n1)</sup>.
- **Singapore:** open; BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training, the government body that registers and clears migrant workers) cleared 66,657 workers for it from January to 7 November 2026, the second destination <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** open, the third destination, with 53,611 cleared from January to 6 November 2026 <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>.
- **The Maldives:** issuing new work visas; 32,408 cleared from January to 7 November 2026 <sup>[N2](#n2), [3](#o3)</sup>.
- **Kuwait:** open for company jobs; closed to new Bangladeshi domestic workers, since Kuwait's June 2026 list of approved countries leaves out Bangladesh and the Bangladesh Embassy attests no women's domestic-worker visas <sup>[4](#o4), [N3](#n3), [N4](#n4)</sup>.
- **Jordan:** open for garment factories and for women in domestic work, closed to new recruits in most other jobs since 1 June 2026 <sup>[N5](#n5), [5](#o5)</sup>.
- **Italy:** only through an employer in Italy who names you under the yearly quota; about 10,000 Bangladeshis got work visas in 2025 <sup>[N6](#n6), [6](#o6)</sup>.
- **The UAE:** work and visit visas for Bangladeshis have been restricted since July 2024, with no published ban <sup>[N7](#n7), [N8](#n8)</sup>.
- **Malaysia:** a restart was announced on 15 October 2026, but the medical tests it needs had not been approved by 4 November 2026 <sup>[7](#o7), [N9](#n9)</sup>.
- **Oman:** new visas for Bangladeshis suspended since 31 October 2023, apart from a few categories <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10), [N11 (2024)](#n11), [N12](#n12)</sup>.
- **Kyrgyzstan:** BMET cleared only 11 workers from January to 9 November 2026 <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
- **Cambodia:** BMET cleared none in October 2026, and the Ministry told BMET not to clear workers for 12 countries, Cambodia among them, without its own approval <sup>[9](#o9), [N13](#n13)</sup>.

Each country doc opens with its current status under "Is it open now, and who can go?" (for example, see the Qatar doc, Background, Status).

<a id="why-wont-bmet-clear-workers-for-some-countries"></a>

#### 1.1.2 Why won't BMET clear workers for some countries?

On 4 November 2026 the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare told BMET not to issue clearances for 12 countries without the Ministry's recruitment permission: Serbia, Moldova, Belarus, Congo, Russia, Japan, Iraq, Laos, Vietnam, Cambodia, Ukraine and Libya <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. The State Minister has named Serbia, Laos, Cambodia, Vietnam and Libya as high-risk countries where visas must be strictly checked <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. In Cambodia, a study by BRAC (the Bangladeshi development organisation) found 98% of returnees got no job and 90% were forced into online scams <sup>[N15](#n15)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the list's Congo actually Kosovo, and is Cambodia on it or Croatia, as other reports of the same 4 November 2026 order give <sup>[N16](#n16), [N13](#n13)</sup>?

<a id="which-countries-can-a-woman-go-to"></a>

#### 1.1.3 Which countries can a woman go to?

- **Saudi Arabia:** as a domestic worker, aged 25 to 45, and she should pay nothing (see [Does a woman going as a domestic worker pay anything?](#does-a-woman-going-as-a-domestic-worker-pay-anything)) <sup>[10](#o10), [11](#o11)</sup>.
- **Jordan:** garment factories, through BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Ltd, the government's recruiting agency); 12,784 women went from January to 8 November 2026, the second destination for women after Saudi Arabia <sup>[12](#o12), [13](#o13)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** domestic work, with the same 25 to 45 age rule for BMET clearance; in August 2025 BMET let one agency send 100 women housemaids to a Qatar employer <sup>[10](#o10), [14](#o14)</sup>.
- **Kuwait:** company jobs only (230 women from January to 8 November 2026); the Bangladesh Embassy attests no women's domestic-worker visas <sup>[15](#o15), [N4](#n4)</sup>.
- **The Maldives:** women can go, but few do (148 in 2026 to 7 November) <sup>[16](#o16)</sup>.
- **Singapore:** almost none: 126 women of 70,832 workers cleared in 2025 <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Oman:** 3 women cleared in 2026 to 8 November, after 6,636 in 2023 <sup>[18](#o18), [19](#o19)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are UAE recruitment offices getting domestic-worker entry permits for Bangladeshi women in November 2026, or are they refused at the visa stage?

<a id="is-there-a-job-for-someone-with-no-skills"></a>

#### 1.1.4 Is there a job for someone with no skills?

Saudi Arabia's skill certificate is not required of domestic workers, though women going to the Middle East for domestic work must take the housekeeping course <sup>[N17](#n17), [10](#o10)</sup>. Saudi Arabia's skill test, the SVP (Skills Verification Program, run by Takamol), is compulsory for listed trades, and the list includes street, hospital and office cleaners, loading and unloading workers and construction workers <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>. Qatar requires a training and test certificate only for professions its labour ministry lists <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. A Singapore construction job needs a skills test at one of five centres in Bangladesh approved by Singapore's Building and Construction Authority, until the test moves to Singapore in January 2028 <sup>[22](#o22), [23](#o23)</sup>. BMET's TTCs (Technical Training Centres) teach 55 trades, and project-funded courses are free and pay a monthly stipend <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="how-much-does-it-really-cost-to-go"></a>

#### 1.1.5 How much does it really cost to go?

People pay several times the legal maximum (see [What is the most an agency may charge me?](#what-is-the-most-an-agency-may-charge-me)): workers who went to five Gulf countries on a "free visa" (a visa bought from a sponsor with no real job behind it) spent Tk 4,99,802 on average, against a government-set average of Tk 1,16,224 <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>. By country:
- **Saudi Arabia:** about Tk 5 lakh on average, up to Tk 9 lakh <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** Tk 4 to 7 lakh for a "free visa" <sup>[N20](#n20)</sup>.
- **Malaysia:** Tk 5.44 lakh on average in 2022-24 <sup>[N21](#n21)</sup>.
- **Singapore:** first-time workers are asked for S$9,000 to 13,000, about Tk 8.7 to 12.6 lakh, and some pay up to Tk 16 lakh <sup>[N22](#n22), [24](#o24), [N23](#n23)</sup>.
- **Kuwait:** a visa is sold through brokers for KD 2,000 to 2,200, about Tk 8 to 9 lakh <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.
- **The UAE:** at least Tk 3.5 lakh for driving jobs, the State Minister said in May 2026 <sup>[N25](#n25)</sup>.
- **The Maldives:** about Tk 3 to 4 lakh in 2024 <sup>[N26 (2024)](#n26)</sup>.
- **Kyrgyzstan:** Tk 5 to 7 lakh a person, by the CID's (Criminal Investigation Department's) 2024 estimate <sup>[N27 (2024)](#n27)</sup>.
- **Italy:** one worker paid an intermediary EUR 20,000 <sup>[N28](#n28)</sup>.
- **Jordan garment jobs through BOESL:** the factory pays; the worker pays Tk 200 for fingerprints and Tk 200 for the orientation, and the company repays these before departure <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>.

<a id="what-is-the-most-an-agency-may-charge-me"></a>

#### 1.1.6 What is the most an agency may charge me?

The government fixes the maximum by order <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. If BMET has issued a permission letter for your job, the most is the amount written on it (see [What is BMET's permission letter](#what-is-bmets-permission-letter-and-where-do-i-find-it), and where do I find it?) <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>. Otherwise the caps reported for each country, with the year each was set or reported, are:
- Saudi Arabia: Tk 1,65,000, set in 2016 <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.
- Singapore: Tk 2,62,270, as reported by Bangladesh Bank in 2019 <sup>[28 (2019)](#o28)</sup>.
- The UAE: Tk 1,07,780, set in 2017 <sup>[29 (2019)](#o29)</sup>.
- Kuwait: Tk 1,06,780, as stated by the Bangladesh Embassy in Kuwait in August 2025 <sup>[N29](#n29)</sup>.
- Malaysia: Tk 78,990, the cap for the 2022-24 recruitment <sup>[N21](#n21)</sup>.
- Italy: none; the 2017 caps don't cover it <sup>[29 (2019)](#o29)</sup>.

A woman going as a domestic worker may be charged nothing <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the Ministry issued the new country-by-country maximums the State Minister promised in October 2026 <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>?

<a id="which-fees-do-i-pay-to-the-government"></a>

#### 1.1.7 Which fees do I pay to the government?

The main Bangladesh-side fees (the full list for Saudi Arabia is in the Saudi doc, Work visa: company worker, Who pays what):
- OEP (Overseas Employment Platform, BMET's website) registration: Tk 200 <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.
- A 48-page, 5-year e-passport: Tk 4,025 (regular) <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>.
- Police clearance: Tk 1,500 on the portal <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.
- Welfare fee to the Wage Earners' Welfare Board: Tk 5,500 from 1 October 2026 <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.
- Migrant insurance: Tk 1,000, once <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>.
- Smart card (clearance) fee: Tk 250 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- Source income tax at clearance: Tk 250 to 1,200, depending on the visa <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="how-much-will-i-earn"></a>

#### 1.1.8 How much will I earn?

- **Saudi Arabia:** cleaners and general workers earn SR 800 to 1,000 a month; there is no minimum wage for foreigners <sup>[N30](#n30), [N31 (2024)](#n31)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** the legal minimum is QR 1,000 a month, plus QR 500 for housing and QR 300 for food if the employer doesn't provide them <sup>[35 (2020)](#o35)</sup>.
- **Malaysia:** the minimum wage is RM1,700 a month <sup>[36 (2024)](#o36)</sup>.
- **Singapore:** construction basic pay averages S$820 a month for Bangladeshis <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.
- **Kuwait:** the Bangladesh Embassy attests a government-contract cleaner's or labourer's visa only if it pays at least KD 90 a month <sup>[37](#o37)</sup>.
- **Jordan garment factories:** JD 230 a month, of which at least JD 130 is cash and JD 100 is paid in kind as housing and food <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>.
- **The Maldives:** migrant workers surveyed in 2024 averaged MVR 6,760 a month, and there is no minimum wage for foreigners <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34), [38 (2021)](#o38)</sup>.
- **Oman:** a minimum of RO 90 a month was agreed between the two governments but is not rigorously enforced <sup>[N35 (2021)](#n35)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-will-it-take-to-pay-back-what-i-spend"></a>

#### 1.1.9 How long will it take to pay back what I spend?

In 2020 men took 19 months or more on average to recover what they spent <sup>[N36 (2020)](#n36)</sup>. A Saudi worker on SR 800 with free housing who buys his own food (SR 500 or more a month) and phone (SR 50 or more) keeps at most SR 250, about Tk 8,300 at Tk 33.20 a riyal <sup>[N30](#n30), [N37](#n37)</sup>; at that rate a Tk 4.5 lakh debt takes about 54 months to repay before interest, against a usual two-year contract <sup>[39](#o39)</sup>. Many low-skilled Gulf workers struggle to send even Tk 15,000 to 20,000 a month; at that rate the same debt takes 23 to 30 months <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38)</sup>. Malaysia workers who paid high fees stayed in debt for two to three years (2024) <sup>[N39 (2024)](#n39)</sup>.

<a id="who-pays-for-the-air-ticket"></a>

#### 1.1.10 Who pays for the air ticket?

It depends on the job. On Saudi jobs with a BMET permission letter, the letter can put the airfare on the employer, as Bonanza Overseas' September 2025 letter does <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>. On BOESL's Jordan garment jobs the factory bears all the costs <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>. On a visa a relative sends from Qatar, the worker usually buys the ticket through the Dhaka broker <sup>[N40 (2017)](#n40)</sup>.

<a id="can-i-go-through-the-government-agency-boesl"></a>

#### 1.1.11 Can I go through the government agency, BOESL?

BOESL has sent 1.43 lakh workers in 14 years, 1.06 lakh of them to Jordan, where it sends women to garment factories at no cost <sup>[N41](#n41), [13](#o13)</sup>. In November 2026 it advertised 112 zero-cost jobs for skilled men at DHL in Malaysia, with applications on OEP <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>. Recruitment under the October 2025 Saudi agreement goes through private agencies, not BOESL <sup>[N42](#n42)</sup>, and BOESL lists no Qatar recruitment <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>.

<a id="agencies-and-dalals"></a>

### 1.2 Agencies and dalals

<a id="how-do-i-check-whether-an-agency-is-real"></a>

#### 1.2.1 How do I check whether an agency is real?

1. Ask for the agency's RL (recruiting licence) number and find it on OEP's agency list at www.oep.gov.bd/agencies, which shows its name, address, office phone, licence status and validity date <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.
2. Read the status, not the date: a licence can show "Cancelled" while its validity date is years ahead <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>. Recruiting after cancellation is a crime carrying 1 to 2 years' prison <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
3. Check the job too, because licensed agencies also cheat: workers paid agencies Tk 3 to 4.2 lakh for Qatar jobs and three of them got fake contract letters <sup>[N43 (2023)](#n43), [N44 (2023)](#n44), [N45 (2023)](#n45)</sup>.

The most active agencies, with their cited red flags, are in the knowledge base's Recruiting agencies doc; the steps for one country are in the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 1.

<a id="is-it-legal-to-go-through-a-dalal"></a>

#### 1.2.2 Is it legal to go through a dalal?

A dalal (দালাল, broker) is legal only if a licensed agency has registered and appointed him as its sub-agent <sup>[44 (2023)](#o44)</sup>. Acting as a sub-agent without registration carries 1 year's prison or a fine of Tk 50,000 to 2 lakh, and the agency that uses one risks 1 year or Tk 1 to 5 lakh <sup>[44 (2023)](#o44)</sup>. Most people still go through one: 56% of 1,084 Gulf migrants in a 2025 OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation) survey relied on informal or illegal agents <sup>[N46](#n46), [N18](#n18)</sup>, and in the Maldives 59% went through a sub-agent or dalal <sup>[N47 (2023)](#n47)</sup>. The broker is often a friend or relative <sup>[N48 (2024)](#n48)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a public list of registered sub-agents that a worker can check a dalal's name against <sup>[44 (2023)](#o44)</sup>?

<a id="what-is-a-demand-letter"></a>

#### 1.2.3 What is a demand letter?

A demand letter is the employer's written request for workers; for Qatar, the company or its agent files it at the Bangladesh Embassy with the government's visa approval, and the Embassy attests it <sup>[45 (2021)](#o45)</sup>. The Bangladesh mission checks the employer and attests the job demand and visa before the agency selects workers <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>. Ask the agency to show you the attested copy (see the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 1, item 4) <sup>[45 (2021)](#o45)</sup>.

<a id="what-is-bmets-permission-letter-and-where-do-i-find-it"></a>

#### 1.2.4 What is BMET's permission letter, and where do I find it?

It is BMET's approval for an agency to send a set number of workers to a named employer; it shows the job, the salary, the contract terms, who pays the migration cost, and the most the agency may take <sup>[14](#o14), [27](#o27)</sup>. It also makes the agency promise, on stamp paper, to pay the worker's food, lodging, return and compensation if the job is not as promised <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>. BMET posts these letters at bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos, but the newest one there is dated 10 December 2025 <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. A single visa (up to eight workers for one employer on one demand letter) needs no Ministry recruitment permission, so it has no such letter <sup>[N49 (2017)](#n49)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does BMET still issue permission letters after 10 December 2025, and can a worker get a copy from BMET <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>?

<a id="a-relative-abroad-is-sending-me-a-visa-is-that-safe"></a>

#### 1.2.5 A relative abroad is sending me a visa. Is that safe?

Many are bought "free visas", sold on by a sponsor who will not employ the worker (see "A free visa lets me work for anyone") <sup>[N40 (2017)](#n40)</sup>. From Cumilla and other districts about 80% go on visas from relatives and friends <sup>[N50 (2024)](#n50)</sup>. Relatives working abroad often get the visa by paying the employer or others and pass it on to people at home <sup>[N51 (2017)](#n51)</sup>. A bought visa also shows as genuine on Qatar's visa checker, so check that the company on the visa is the one you will work for and that the job matches <sup>[N52](#n52)</sup>. Buying or selling a visa inside Bangladesh carries 2 to 7 years' prison <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. What a relative may be sending is explained in the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 1, item 5.

<a id="how-should-i-pay-the-agency-and-when"></a>

#### 1.2.6 How should I pay the agency, and when?

Pay through the bank into the agency's own account and keep every receipt <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. BMET asks people not to go to middlemen and to pay only through the proper channel against a receipt <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. BMET's September 2025 permission letter for Saudi kitchen workers, for example, let the agency take its fee only by cheque, bank draft or pay order, and only after the visa, clearance and flight were confirmed <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>. In practice most workers pay sub-agents in instalments before the visa is confirmed and get the contract only at the airport <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.

<a id="is-there-a-list-of-good-agencies"></a>

#### 1.2.7 Is there a list of good agencies?

One agency has outside evidence of fair recruitment: Bonanza Overseas (RL-0579) was the Bangladeshi agency in a fair-recruitment pilot that the ILO (International Labour Organization) began in 2018 with QDVC, a construction company in Qatar <sup>[N53 (2022)](#n53)</sup>. The Recruiting agencies doc lists the most active agencies with their red flags.

<a id="the-agency-took-my-money-and-didnt-send-me-what-can-i-do"></a>

#### 1.2.8 The agency took my money and didn't send me. What can I do?

1. Write down who took the money, from whom, when and how much; a Ministry joint secretary said in 2025 that action follows when these are given <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>.
2. Apply to BMET's Director General with your proof at the workers' complaint cell; it is free and meant to be settled within 30 working days <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
3. Or send an application with proof, your address and the agency's RL number to the Ministry's enforcement branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd; action starts within 3 to 5 working days <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.
4. Mobile courts give immediate remedies to people about to go and returned migrants, free, within 7 working days <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.
5. If nothing happens, go up BMET's grievance ladder to the Ministry's Secretary and then the Cabinet Division's system at www.grs.gov.bd <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

The full route is in the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Scams, If the agency cheats you.

<a id="is-cheating-a-migrant-a-crime"></a>

#### 1.2.9 Is cheating a migrant a crime?

Yes. Taking money on a false promise of a job abroad, keeping a migrant's passport or papers, or luring someone with false promises of higher pay carries up to 5 years' prison and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. Publishing an overseas job advert without government or BMET approval carries up to 1 year <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. Migrants and people cheated in the name of migration have a right to legal aid <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>; OKUP gives free legal advice and help filing cases on 01842773300 <sup>[N55](#n55)</sup>.

<a id="papers"></a>

### 1.3 Papers

<a id="how-do-i-get-a-passport-and-what-does-it-cost"></a>

#### 1.3.1 How do I get a passport, and what does it cost?

Apply for an e-passport exactly as your NID (national ID card) reads; anyone over 20 must use the NID <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. A 48-page, 5-year e-passport costs Tk 4,025 (regular), Tk 6,325 (express) or Tk 8,625 (super express) <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>. Pay online through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay, or at a bank <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>, and bring the original NID to the passport office <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. Delivery after biometrics is within 15 working days (regular), 7 (express) or 2 (super express) <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>.

<a id="how-much-validity-must-my-passport-have"></a>

#### 1.3.2 How much validity must my passport have?

It depends on the step: the police clearance needs at least 3 months <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>, and the Qatar Visa Center (QVC, where Qatar-bound workers sign the contract, give fingerprints and take the medical) books only passports with more than 195 days left <sup>[N56 (2018)](#n56), [50](#o50)</sup>. Immigration's hotline gets calls from people asking whether four months is enough to travel <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>. Since 19 August 2026 the Special Branch runs a 24-hour immigration hotline at Shahjalal airport, 01320007588, for questions on passport validity and travel bans <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.

<a id="how-do-i-get-a-police-clearance-certificate"></a>

#### 1.3.3 How do I get a police clearance certificate?

Apply at pcc.police.gov.bd: register, fill in your details and addresses, upload the scans and submit <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. Pay Tk 1,500 by treasury challan at Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank, or online by card <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. The police verify you at your present address; track it by texting "PCC S \<reference number\>" to 26969, and collect it with a copy of your passport <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. The PCC helpdesk is 01320001824 or 01320001825 <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. The full steps are in the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 2.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the fee Tk 1,500 (the portal) or Tk 500 (Bangladesh Police's guidance) <sup>[32](#o32), [51](#o51)</sup>?

<a id="what-is-attestation-what-is-an-apostille-and-which-do-i-need"></a>

#### 1.3.4 What is attestation, what is an apostille, and which do I need?

An apostille is a single certificate from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs (MoFA) that member countries of the Hague Apostille Convention accept; it is applied for on mygov.bd at Tk 200 a page and takes 5 working days <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. MoFA lists 114 countries that accept it, including Oman, Saudi Arabia and Italy, but not Qatar <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>. For a country that doesn't accept it, each document is first verified by its issuing body and then attested by MoFA: SSC and HSC (Secondary School Certificate and Higher Secondary Certificate) certificates by the Education Board, then the Ministry of Education, then MoFA <sup>[54](#o54), [55](#o55)</sup>. The Qatar steps, including legalisation by the Qatari mission, are in the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 2, item 3; the apostille steps in the Italy doc, Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal), Step 4.

<a id="how-do-i-register-on-oep"></a>

#### 1.3.5 How do I register on OEP?

Registration on OEP is the first step: agencies must pick workers from BMET's database, and BMET clears no one outside it <sup>[14](#o14), [46](#o46)</sup>. Open employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp in Google Chrome, type your details exactly as on the NID, enter the code sent to your mobile, fill in the form, and pay by bKash, Nagad or another mobile wallet <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. The fee is Tk 200 <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>. Download your Registration Card at the end <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. If it fails, call the OEP hotline, 09614445271 <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>.

<a id="what-is-the-medical-and-how-do-i-book-it"></a>

#### 1.3.6 What is the medical, and how do I book it?

For the Gulf countries it is the Wafid check, booked online and done at a medical centre assigned by your city; people still call it "GAMCA", after the system Wafid replaced <sup>[58](#o58), [N58](#n58)</sup>. Book the slip yourself at wafid.com: USD 10 for a centre assigned to you, or USD 25 to choose the centre and date <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. The whole medical should cost no more than Tk 8,500 to 9,000 <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>, yet most workers pay at least Tk 17,000 to 20,000 <sup>[N60](#n60)</sup>. Check the result on wafid.com's status search; it can take up to 26 days <sup>[58](#o58)</sup>. Qatar-bound workers take their medical at the QVC instead (see the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 4); the Wafid steps are in the Saudi doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 4.

<a id="can-i-pay-to-be-passed-fit-in-the-medical"></a>

#### 1.3.7 Can I pay to be passed "fit" in the medical?

The Daily Star reported in July 2025 that agencies offer "fit" packages, Tk 20,000 against a Tk 10,000 standard one <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>, and that a syndicate reportedly charges Tk 25,000 to 30,000 for a guaranteed fit certificate <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>. A worker who paid Tk 25,000 for one was found unfit in Saudi Arabia and deported <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>. The same report said the approved centres are allegedly controlled by a few owners who issue fake certificates <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>.

<a id="what-if-im-declared-unfit"></a>

#### 1.3.8 What if I'm declared unfit?

You can't retake the Wafid test for three months <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>. If you think the result is wrong or the centre demanded extra money, complain in writing to the Gulf Health Council within two weeks of the test; anonymous complaints are refused <sup>[60 (2021)](#o60)</sup>. Overcharging can also be reported to the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare's enforcement branch with your receipts and the centre's name and address (see the Saudi doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 4) <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>.

<a id="do-i-need-a-skill-test"></a>

#### 1.3.9 Do I need a skill test?

- **Saudi Arabia:** the SVP is compulsory for listed trades; it costs USD 85 per attempt, runs at 27 TTCs and BRTC Gazipur (the Bangladesh Road Transport Corporation's training institute), and a score of 50% passes <sup>[62](#o62), [N30](#n30), [N62 (2023)](#n62)</sup>. Steps: the Saudi doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 3.
- **Singapore construction:** a skills test at a Bangladesh test centre until January 2028, when workers will be tested in Singapore after arrival <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** only for professions on the labour ministry's list <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.
- **Domestic workers:** no Saudi skill test, but women must take the housekeeping course (see [What is the housekeeping course?](#what-is-the-housekeeping-course)) <sup>[N17](#n17), [10](#o10)</sup>.

There is no way to pass the Saudi test by paying extra, and a worker caught paying has the account locked and can't go to Saudi Arabia <sup>[63](#o63), [64](#o64)</sup>.

<a id="how-do-i-join-a-ttc-course"></a>

#### 1.3.10 How do I join a TTC course?

Log in on OEP, open Training portal, then Training, then Trade/Skills, choose your division, district and centre, pick the trade and press Apply <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. Pay the BMET fee for short courses against a money receipt; project-run courses are free and pay a stipend, and courses last from 3 days to 4 years <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. If anyone offers to get you into or through a TTC for money, tell the nearest law-enforcement office <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>.

<a id="what-is-the-pre-departure-orientation-pdo"></a>

#### 1.3.11 What is the pre-departure orientation (PDO)?

It is a compulsory 3-day course at one of 70 training institutes, booked on OEP; the institute sets the fee and gives a money receipt <sup>[10](#o10), [34](#o34)</sup>. Booked through the Ami Probashi phone app, the enrolment fee is Tk 100 plus a Tk 100 platform charge <sup>[N63](#n63)</sup>.

<a id="how-do-i-check-my-visa-is-real"></a>

#### 1.3.12 How do I check my visa is real?

Take copies of the visa and the passport's visa page to BMET in Dhaka or your district employment and manpower office (DEMO) <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>; BMET checks it free within 3 working days <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. BMET checks Saudi, Qatar, Bahrain, Singapore and UAE work visas online, and has a visa-check app on Google Play <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. The State Minister said in 2026 that BMET can verify visas online for only four or five countries <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. Each country doc gives its own online check (for example, see the Saudi doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 7).

<a id="bmet-clearance-and-the-smart-card"></a>

### 1.4 BMET clearance and the smart card

<a id="what-is-the-smart-card-and-do-i-need-it"></a>

#### 1.4.1 What is the smart card, and do I need it?

The smart card is BMET's emigration clearance, and welfare desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet airports check it before departure <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. It also makes you a member of the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (WEWB, the Ministry's welfare body for migrants), whose members get help when sick, easier loans from Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB), scholarships for their children and Tk 3 lakh for the heirs if they die <sup>[65 (2021)](#o65), [66](#o66)</sup>. It gives you the migrant insurance too <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Families of workers without clearance or membership don't get the Welfare Board's death grant <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.

<a id="how-do-i-get-bmet-clearance"></a>

#### 1.4.2 How do I get BMET clearance?

- **Through an agency:** the agency applies after the visa, with the visa copy, the attested contract, your registration card and the fees; BMET clears within 3 working days <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- **On a visa you got yourself:** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram district employment and manpower office with the visa copy, the contract if any, your registration card, a declaration on Tk 300 stamp paper and the fees; 3 working days <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

Since July 2023 the application can also be made online through the Ami Probashi phone app, and the card downloaded, without going to BMET <sup>[N64 (2023)](#n64)</sup>. The full list of papers is in the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 6, item 3.

<a id="how-much-does-clearance-cost"></a>

#### 1.4.3 How much does clearance cost?

- Welfare fee: Tk 5,500 from 1 October 2026, up from Tk 3,500 <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.
- Insurance premium: Tk 1,000, once <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>.
- Smart card fee: Tk 250 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- Source income tax: Tk 400 on a single visa not attested by the embassy, Tk 250 if attested, and Tk 1,200 (skilled) or Tk 800 (unskilled) on a group visa <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>; Tk 500 on a Saudi single visa <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-does-clearance-take"></a>

#### 1.4.4 How long does clearance take?

BMET's charter promises clearance within 3 working days of a complete application, whether the agency applies or you do <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="how-do-i-check-that-my-smart-card-is-real"></a>

#### 1.4.5 How do I check that my smart card is real?

Check it at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>. Airport welfare desks checked 1,391,988 cards in 2023-24 and stopped 1,098 travellers with no or a fake clearance <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>. If something is wrong, contact BMET's Director (Emigration), 01711-135901, diremi@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="do-i-have-to-pay-a-bribe-to-get-clearance"></a>

#### 1.4.6 Do I have to pay a bribe to get clearance?

BMET's charter lists only the fees above, and BMET asks anyone offered clearance for money to tell the nearest law-enforcement office <sup>[10](#o10), [34](#o34)</sup>. The State Minister said in 2026 that some dishonest BMET officials give clearance on fake papers <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. A Home Ministry additional secretary admitted that some complaints of passengers paying police at the airport to get through are true <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

<a id="i-went-abroad-without-clearance-can-i-still-join-the-welfare-board"></a>

#### 1.4.7 I went abroad without clearance. Can I still join the Welfare Board?

Yes. Apply online at portal.wewb.gov.bd; the application is processed through the Bangladesh embassy or high commission <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>. Members get a card with a QR code and the Board's benefits <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>. The steps for Qatar, including the fee, are in the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, Step 7, Welfare Board sign-up if you left without BMET clearance.

<a id="loans"></a>

### 1.5 Loans

<a id="can-i-get-a-government-loan-to-go-abroad"></a>

#### 1.5.1 Can I get a government loan to go abroad?

PKB, the government bank for migrant workers, lends up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% simple interest, repaid monthly over up to 3 years including a grace period, and decides within 7 working days of complete papers <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>. Women migrants have a separate loan on the same terms <sup>[71](#o71)</sup>. You must be 18 to 60, not in default, and live permanently in the branch's area with your guarantors <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>. You need two guarantors, one financially solvent and one family member <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>. Helpline: 16238 <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>.

<a id="when-can-i-apply-for-the-pkb-loan"></a>

#### 1.5.2 When can I apply for the PKB loan?

Only once you hold the visa and the BMET smart card, near the end of the process <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>. The bank doesn't arrange visas, and finding guarantors stops many people from borrowing <sup>[N65 (2023)](#n65)</sup>. You can apply at the branch or online on mygov.bd, paying nothing when you submit; processing and documentation fees of 0.5% each come after the decision <sup>[72 (2019)](#o72)</sup>.

<a id="should-i-borrow-from-a-moneylender"></a>

#### 1.5.3 Should I borrow from a moneylender?

In a 2020 government survey, 81% of men had borrowed to go abroad <sup>[N66 (2021)](#n66)</sup>. In a 2020 IOM (International Organization for Migration) survey, most family loans were interest-free, NGO and bank loans carried 10-15%, and 62% of moneylender debt carried 50-150% interest <sup>[N67 (2020)](#n67)</sup>. A microcredit NGO may charge at most 24% a year <sup>[N68 (2024)](#n68)</sup>. A quarter of borrowers risk losing the collateral they pledged if they can't repay <sup>[73 (2020)](#o73)</sup>, and families mortgage land and sell cattle: a Natore farmer mortgaged his land and sold four cows, then could not fly <sup>[N69 (2024)](#n69)</sup>. A former expatriates' welfare secretary said what workers send home often doesn't even cover the interest <sup>[N70 (2023)](#n70)</sup>.

<a id="can-i-borrow-abroad"></a>

#### 1.5.4 Can I borrow abroad?

In Singapore, a worker found borrowing from an unlicensed moneylender has his Work Permit revoked and is sent home <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>. Many workers in Malaysia borrow at 10% a month to renew their permits, an economist of the CPD (Centre for Policy Dialogue, a Dhaka think tank) said in October 2025 <sup>[N71](#n71)</sup>.

<a id="at-the-airport"></a>

### 1.6 At the airport

<a id="what-should-i-carry"></a>

#### 1.6.1 What should I carry?

Before you go, check you have <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>:
- a valid passport;
- the work visa;
- the medical certificate;
- the demand letter;
- the contract attested by the destination country's embassy;
- the agency's name, address and phone;
- the BMET smart card;
- the air ticket.

Keep one set of copies at home and at least three with you <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Never carry a packet for anyone whose contents you don't know: drug offences abroad can carry the death penalty <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>.

<a id="what-does-immigration-ask"></a>

#### 1.6.2 What does immigration ask?

Immigration police ask about your purpose and family, and they compare your visa, BMET clearance, papers and answers <sup>[N72 (2021)](#n72), [N14](#n14)</sup>. A woman with BMET clearance who said she was joining her husband in Oman was questioned closely, because Oman's general worker visas and women's domestic-worker recruitment are closed, and was stopped when the same spouse-visa approval turned out to have sent five women to Oman <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. The Special Branch's 24-hour immigration hotline at Shahjalal airport is 01320007588 <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.

<a id="why-do-people-get-offloaded"></a>

#### 1.6.3 Why do people get offloaded?

Immigration police can stop a traveller they doubt from flying and stamp "offload" in the passport <sup>[N72 (2021)](#n72)</sup>. The usual reasons are a visa that doesn't match the BMET clearance, fake papers, a breach of visa conditions, or answers that don't match the papers <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. The Special Branch turned back 81,500 passengers from 2021 to June 2026, 7,819 of them in the first half of 2026 <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. People with valid papers have been stopped too: a man with a valid Malaysia visa and BMET clearance was turned back and then let through after a senior officer intervened <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. A Home Ministry additional secretary admitted that some complaints of people stopped unfairly are true <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

<a id="who-helps-me-at-the-airport"></a>

#### 1.6.4 Who helps me at the airport?

WEWB's welfare desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet airports check smart cards, help returnees with customs and lost luggage, send the sick to hospital by ambulance and take complaints from abused or cheated returnees <sup>[10](#o10), [75](#o75)</sup>. The Wage Earners' Centre near Shahjalal airport gives departing and returning workers a short stay; book at wec.wewb.gov.bd <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

<a id="in-the-destination"></a>

### 1.7 In the destination

<a id="my-salary-isnt-paid-what-do-i-do"></a>

#### 1.7.1 My salary isn't paid. What do I do?

The routes:
- **Qatar:** complain to the labour ministry, which must first try to settle the dispute; a Qatari official advised complaining within a month, because 3 to 4 months' unpaid wages become hard to recover <sup>[21](#o21), [N73 (2024)](#n73)</sup>. An unpaid worker may ask to change employer or end the contract, with the journey home at the employer's cost <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.
- **Saudi Arabia:** file a friendly-settlement claim with the labour ministry within 12 months; unsettled claims go to the labour court <sup>[77](#o77), [78](#o78)</sup>. After 3 months unpaid you may move to another employer without consent <sup>[N74](#n74)</sup>.
- **Every country:** your family can apply to the Ministry's Welfare-1 branch in Dhaka with proof of your status, the employer's name and address and the amounts owed; it is free and the Ministry writes to the labour wing within 7 working days <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.

The steps for each country are under "If you aren't paid" in its doc (for example, see the Qatar doc, Work visa: company worker, If something goes wrong, If you aren't paid, are abused or your passport is taken).

<a id="my-employer-has-taken-my-passport-is-that-allowed"></a>

#### 1.7.2 My employer has taken my passport. Is that allowed?

No, though it is common:
- **Qatar:** the employer must return it once the residence permit is done, unless you ask in writing for the employer to keep it, and hand it over whenever you ask; keeping it is punishable by a fine of up to QR 25,000 <sup>[79 (2015)](#o79)</sup>.
- **Saudi Arabia:** your passport and iqama (residence card) may never be withheld <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>.
- **The UAE:** passport confiscation is banned, and you need no employer permission to leave the country <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.
- **Kuwait:** keeping it is illegal but widespread; complain to the PAM (Public Authority for Manpower) office where your residence was stamped <sup>[N75](#n75), [81](#o81)</sup>.
- **Singapore:** employers should not keep workers' passports, yet over 31% of Work Permit holders didn't hold their own in 2024 <sup>[82](#o82), [83](#o83)</sup>.

In Bangladesh, withholding a migrant's passport without lawful reason carries up to 5 years' prison <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.

<a id="can-i-change-my-employer"></a>

#### 1.7.3 Can I change my employer?

- **Qatar:** since 2020 the law lets you move without the employer's consent, under the labour ministry's rules <sup>[84 (2020)](#o84)</sup>.
- **Saudi Arabia:** after 12 months, with 90 days' notice, if your contract is on Qiwa (the labour ministry's platform); sooner if you go 3 months unpaid or your work permit or visa expires <sup>[85 (2020)](#o85), [N74](#n74)</sup>.
- **The UAE:** transfers between companies have been restricted for Bangladeshis since July 2024 <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.
- **Kuwait:** normally after one year and with the employer's consent <sup>[N77](#n77)</sup>.
- **Malaysia:** the PLKS (Temporary Employment Visit Pass, the work pass) doesn't let you change employer or sector <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.
- **Singapore:** you may work only for the employer and in the job on your Work Permit <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>.
- **Jordan garment factories:** only to another garment factory <sup>[88](#o88)</sup>.
- **Kyrgyzstan:** the permit can't be moved to another employer <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>.
- **Italy:** losing or leaving the job doesn't cancel your permit <sup>[90](#o90)</sup>.

In Saudi Arabia you may not be employed in a profession other than the one on your work permit <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>.

<a id="i-arrived-and-there-is-no-job-or-the-visa-was-fake-what-now"></a>

#### 1.7.4 I arrived and there is no job, or the visa was fake. What now?

This is the most common failure of a bought visa: in OKUP's 2025 survey, 21% of free-visa workers paid about Tk 1.5 lakh more after arriving to get a work permit, and 14% never got one and stayed on illegally <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>. BMET says 90% of migrants' complaints in 2025 concerned Saudi Arabia, mostly no iqama, no work or unpaid wages <sup>[N78](#n78)</sup>. The embassy can advise and help in a labour dispute (in Qatar, for example) <sup>[91 (2021)](#o91)</sup>, and your family can complain about the agency in Bangladesh (see [The agency took my money and didn't send me](#the-agency-took-my-money-and-didnt-send-me-what-can-i-do). What can I do?) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. If you can't get home, a relative can ask the Ministry for help to bring you back <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.

<a id="what-happens-if-i-overstay"></a>

#### 1.7.5 What happens if I overstay?

- **Saudi Arabia:** without a valid iqama you are undocumented, and police detain and deport undocumented workers who go out <sup>[N79 (2024)](#n79)</sup>; overstaying a visit or Umrah visa brings a 3 to 5 year ban <sup>[N80](#n80)</sup>.
- **The UAE:** AED 50 a day after the grace period that follows the end of a residence permit <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** overstaying a visit visa costs QR 200 a day, up to QR 12,000 <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.
- **Cambodia:** USD 10 a day, and deportation can take more than six months <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>.
- **Kyrgyzstan:** a fine, and an expulsion brings a 2-year entry ban <sup>[94](#o94), [95](#o95)</sup>.

Countries sometimes open grace periods: Qatar gave three months from 9 February 2025 for overstayers to leave <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>. How to get home is under "Going home without valid papers" in each country doc.

<a id="how-do-i-send-money-home-safely"></a>

#### 1.7.6 How do I send money home safely?

Use a bank, a licensed exchange house or a mobile wallet such as bKash, not hundi (unlicensed money agents) <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Money sent through legal channels earns the receiver a 2.5% government cash incentive, sometimes with another 2.5% from the bank, and the 2026-27 budget keeps it <sup>[96](#o96), [97](#o97)</sup>. Sending more at once costs less: in April to June 2026 the average cost was 2.94% for up to USD 200 and 0.50% above USD 500 <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. For bKash, give the partner branch the receiver's bKash number and full registered name <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>. Banks in Bangladesh credit remittances within 3 days at most <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>. The channels for each country are in its doc, under Sending money home.

<a id="is-hundi-illegal"></a>

#### 1.7.7 Is hundi illegal?

Yes, in Bangladesh sending money outside banking channels is a punishable offence <sup>[N83 (2022)](#n83)</sup>. Abroad it can cost you the job: in Singapore a worker involved in hundi has his permit revoked <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>.

<a id="who-can-i-call-for-help-while-abroad"></a>

#### 1.7.8 Who can I call for help while abroad?

- Probash-Bondhu, WEWB's 24-hour call centre: 16135 free in Bangladesh, +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.
- The Ministry's Complaint Resolution Cell: minister.expat@gmail.com <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.
- The Bangladesh embassy's labour wing in your country (for example, in Saudi Arabia, 8001000125 free) <sup>[N84](#n84)</sup>.

The labour wing's numbers are under Contacts in each country doc.

<a id="women"></a>

### 1.8 Women

<a id="can-a-woman-go-abroad-for-work-without-her-guardians-permission"></a>

#### 1.8.1 Can a woman go abroad for work without her guardian's permission?

For domestic work, no: BMET clears a woman domestic worker only with proof that she is 25 to 45 and a no-objection letter from her legal guardian <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. A woman going on a single visa also needs her guardian's no-objection letter on stamp paper <sup>[102 (2021)](#o102)</sup>. The housekeeping course asks for the same letter <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>.

<a id="who-can-go-as-a-domestic-worker"></a>

#### 1.8.2 Who can go as a domestic worker?

A woman aged 25 to 45 when she enrols in the housekeeping course (returnees up to 50), who has passed class 5 (which can be relaxed) and is fit <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>. She can't enrol if she is pregnant, if her youngest child is under 6, or if she has a contagious or incurable disease <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>.

<a id="what-is-the-housekeeping-course"></a>

#### 1.8.3 What is the housekeeping course?

It is compulsory for women going to the Middle East for domestic work: one month (24 working days), residential, at one of 41 TTCs <sup>[10](#o10), [103](#o103)</sup>. Apply on OEP's training portal and pay the set fee <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>. You must attend 90% of sessions and score 70% in the final test, and the course teaches how to contact the police and the Bangladesh embassy abroad <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>. The certificate is valid two years for a first departure <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>. Brokers have sold course certificates for money <sup>[N85 (2018)](#n85)</sup>.

<a id="does-a-woman-going-as-a-domestic-worker-pay-anything"></a>

#### 1.8.4 Does a woman going as a domestic worker pay anything?

No. An agency approved to send women domestic workers may take no migration cost from her <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>, and Saudi recruitment offices may take no commission from any domestic worker <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. The Saudi household pays its recruitment office up to SR 11,750 for a Bangladeshi worker <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>. Yet none of the returnee women IOM interviewed in 2020 had gone free <sup>[N87 (2020)](#n87)</sup>.

<a id="which-agencies-may-send-women-domestic-workers"></a>

#### 1.8.5 Which agencies may send women domestic workers?

Only agencies the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare has approved, each with a Tk 15 lakh security deposit and a woman on the staff; the September 2025 order approved 21 <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Ask for the approval letter and check the agency's name and RL number are in it (see the Saudi doc, Work visa: domestic worker, Step 2) <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Ministry publish one current list of all agencies approved to send women domestic workers?

<a id="the-agent-wants-to-change-my-age-on-the-passport-is-that-a-problem"></a>

#### 1.8.6 The agent wants to change my age on the passport. Is that a problem?

Yes: agents have raised girls' ages with a second birth certificate, in one case giving a girl under 13 a passport showing 27 <sup>[N88 (2020)](#n88)</sup>. Check the birth record your passport will use at everify.bdris.gov.bd with the 17-digit registration number and your real date of birth <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. The Probashi call centre, 16135 (free from Bangladesh), gives workers and families advice and help <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

<a id="what-are-a-domestic-workers-rights"></a>

#### 1.8.7 What are a domestic worker's rights?

Saudi Arabia's rules are an example:
- at most 10 hours' work a day, with at least 8 hours' continuous rest <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>;
- one paid day off a week <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>;
- the employer may not keep your passport, papers or belongings <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>;
- the employer must let you contact your family and the embassy <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>.

The rules for other countries are in each country doc's domestic-worker section (for example, see the Qatar doc, Work visa: domestic worker, Your rights at work).

<a id="what-if-im-abused-where-can-i-go"></a>

#### 1.8.8 What if I'm abused? Where can I go?

In Saudi Arabia, the embassy labour wing's free line is 8001000125, and the labour wings run safe homes for women in Riyadh, Jeddah and Madinah <sup>[N84](#n84), [69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>. The labour ministry's abuse line, 1919, takes reports round the clock in confidence <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>. A relative in Bangladesh can apply to the Ministry's Welfare-2 branch (aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd) for help or to bring you home; it is free and the Ministry acts within 7 working days <sup>[61](#o61)</sup>. Women back within a year after abuse or fraud get Tk 5,000 in cash at the airport welfare desk <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="can-women-get-garment-jobs-in-jordan"></a>

#### 1.8.9 Can women get garment jobs in Jordan?

Yes, through BOESL: its November 2026 notice took women aged 20 to 35 who can run plain and overlock sewing machines, at no cost to the worker <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>. In June 2026 the CID arrested three people who used a Jordan garment company's name and seal to take Tk 2 to 2.5 lakh a person without a licence <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>. Steps: see the Jordan doc, Work visa: garment and factory worker.

<a id="problems-and-coming-home"></a>

### 1.9 Problems and coming home

<a id="what-does-the-migrant-insurance-cover"></a>

#### 1.9.1 What does the migrant insurance cover?

Workers who left with BMET clearance from 1 January 2023 are insured for Tk 10 lakh by Jiban Bima Corporation (JBC), for workers aged 18 to 55, for a one-time Tk 1,000 <sup>[109 (2024)](#o109)</sup>. The cover lasts 5 years from the smart card's date <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. It pays the full Tk 10 lakh on death or total permanent disability, 10% to 90% for partial disability, and Tk 50,000 if you lose the job and return within 6 months of going <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Job loss is not covered if you went entirely at the employer's cost <sup>[109 (2024)](#o109)</sup>. WEWB helps the worker or family claim <sup>[110](#o110)</sup>.

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get"></a>

#### 1.9.2 If a worker dies abroad, what does the family get?

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-1"></a>**At the airport:** a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial for every body that arrives, free, collected within 3 months of the body's arrival <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-2"></a>**From the Welfare Board:** a Tk 3 lakh grant, if the worker had BMET clearance or membership and died in the country the clearance was for <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>. If the family took the airport cheque, no separate application is needed; if the body was buried abroad, apply within 1 year <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-3"></a>**From the insurance:** Tk 10 lakh <sup>[109 (2024)](#o109)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-4"></a>**From the employer:** labour wings recover death compensation, wages and benefits, and the Board pays the heirs; 1,852 cases brought in Tk 127.6 crore in 2023-24 <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>.

The grant goes to the spouse, children and parents <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>. The full steps are in the Qatar doc, If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job.

<a id="how-is-the-body-brought-home"></a>

#### 1.9.3 How is the body brought home?

The family applies to the Welfare Board, which takes their wish (body home or burial abroad) to the embassy and follows the case, free <sup>[112](#o112), [113](#o113)</sup>. The Ministry also brings home bodies of irregular, undocumented and destitute workers: the next of kin applies with the cause of death, proof of status and the death certificate; free, 7 working days <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>. A free ambulance from Dhaka and Chattogram airports is available on request <sup>[114](#o114)</sup>.

<a id="why-do-so-many-families-get-no-compensation-from-the-employer"></a>

#### 1.9.4 Why do so many families get no compensation from the employer?

Many deaths are recorded as "natural causes": 71% of deaths of Bangladeshis in Qatar from November 2016 to October 2020, by Amnesty International's reading of Bangladeshi government records <sup>[N90 (2021)](#n90)</sup>. Amnesty found in 2021 that without investigation or post-mortem, this rules out compensation from employers <sup>[N90 (2021)](#n90)</sup>. The Welfare Board's grant is paid anyway <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.

<a id="what-help-is-there-if-im-injured-or-fall-ill-abroad"></a>

#### 1.9.5 What help is there if I'm injured or fall ill abroad?

The Welfare Board asks the embassy to bring a sick or injured worker home on the worker's or family's application <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>. Back home, workers with clearance or membership can get up to Tk 2 lakh for treatment, applying within 1 year of return <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>. The insurance pays for permanent disability (see [What does the migrant insurance cover?](#what-does-the-migrant-insurance-cover)) <sup>[109 (2024)](#o109)</sup>.

<a id="what-help-is-there-when-i-come-back"></a>

#### 1.9.6 What help is there when I come back?

Airport welfare desks give food and water to returnees from jail, help with customs and lost luggage, and take complaints <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>. Women back within a year after abuse or fraud get Tk 5,000 in cash <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. PKB's rehabilitation loan lends returnees up to Tk 50 lakh for a project at 9% simple interest, up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>. It needs a trade licence and a guarantor, and in 2021 only about 2% of pandemic returnees got PKB's special reintegration loan <sup>[117](#o117), [N91 (2021)](#n91)</sup>.

<a id="what-can-i-bring-home-without-paying-duty"></a>

#### 1.9.7 What can I bring home without paying duty?

Under the Baggage Rules 2025 a returning worker aged 12 or over may bring up to 65 kg free, plus 35 kg more of clothes and personal items <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>. One of each of a list of goods is duty-free, including a laptop, an oven, a rice cooker, a fan and a TV up to 29 inches <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>. You may bring 2 used mobile phones and 1 new one, the new one once a year <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. Bigger items such as a fridge or air conditioner pay a fixed duty <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>. A Bangladeshi passport holder may bring no alcohol <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>. The full list is in the Qatar doc, Coming home, What you can bring home.

<a id="how-much-gold-and-cash-can-i-bring"></a>

#### 1.9.8 How much gold and cash can I bring?

Up to 100 g of gold or 200 g of silver jewellery in all, declared, once a year, with no more than 12 pieces of one type, is duty-free <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>. One gold bar of up to 117 g (10 tola) is allowed once a year if declared, at Tk 5,000 duty per 11.664 g; anything more, or a hidden bar, is confiscated <sup>[118](#o118), [119](#o119)</sup>. Up to USD 10,000 in foreign currency needs no declaration <sup>[120 (2020)](#o120)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do customs officers still apply USD 5,000, the figure on Bangladesh Customs' own page <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>?

<a id="is-there-help-for-my-children"></a>

#### 1.9.9 Is there help for my children?

The Welfare Board pays scholarships to migrants' high-achieving children (Tk 27,500 a year at SSC level, Tk 34,000 at HSC level) and reserves 0.5% of HSC admission seats for them <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>.

<a id="myths"></a>

### 1.10 Myths

<a id="a-free-visa-lets-me-work-for-anyone"></a>

#### 1.10.1 "A free visa lets me work for anyone."

Brokers sell it as "go and work wherever you like," for Tk 5 to 7 lakh <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. In fact a Saudi employer may not employ a worker in a profession other than the one on the work permit <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>, and buying and working on a free visa is illegal and punishable in Qatar <sup>[65 (2021)](#o65)</sup>. The company named can cancel the visa at any time, making the worker illegal <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>. Of 10 men one Cumilla broker sent to Saudi Arabia, three got a proper job with a contract (2024) <sup>[N94 (2024)](#n94)</sup>.

<a id="a-visit-visa-can-be-changed-to-a-work-visa-there"></a>

#### 1.10.2 "A visit visa can be changed to a work visa there."

- **Saudi Arabia:** a visit visa can't be converted to an iqama, and offers to convert one for a fee are fraud <sup>[N95 (2022)](#n95)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** you must leave and re-enter on a work visa <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
- **Oman:** conversion inside the country stopped for all nationalities on 31 October 2023 <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>.
- **The UAE:** a visit visa gives no right to work, and working on one brings fines <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>.

Working on a tourist or Umrah visa in Saudi Arabia brings a ban of 3 to 10 years <sup>[N80](#n80)</sup>.

<a id="the-agency-guarantees-the-job"></a>

#### 1.10.3 "The agency guarantees the job."

The Bangladesh High Commission in the Maldives warned in October 2026 against paying anyone who promises a guaranteed job or a quick work permit <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. A Bangladeshi construction worker in the UAE told a 2019 study that the contract signed in Bangladesh differs from the one the company keeps <sup>[N96 (2019)](#n96)</sup>. In Cambodia, 98% of returnees BRAC studied found no job <sup>[N15](#n15)</sup>.

<a id="pay-a-little-extra-and-the-medical-will-be-fit"></a>

#### 1.10.4 "Pay a little extra and the medical will be fit."

A bought "fit" result can still get you deported: a worker who paid Tk 25,000 for one was found unfit in Saudi Arabia and sent home <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>.

<a id="pay-extra-and-youll-pass-the-skill-test"></a>

#### 1.10.5 "Pay extra and you'll pass the skill test."

There is no way to pass Saudi Arabia's SVP for extra money; a worker caught paying has the account locked <sup>[63](#o63), [64](#o64)</sup>.

<a id="saudi-arabia-abolished-the-kafala"></a>

#### 1.10.6 "Saudi Arabia abolished the kafala."

The kafala (sponsorship system) still stands: the October 2025 story began in an article by a UK think tank and cited no Saudi source <sup>[N97](#n97)</sup>.

<a id="the-iqama-is-the-workers-cost"></a>

#### 1.10.7 "The iqama is the worker's cost."

The employer pays the iqama, the work permit and their renewals <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>. A Bangladesh embassy official confirmed the employer should pay, though employers push it onto workers <sup>[N98](#n98)</sup>.

<a id="if-i-have-a-job-my-visa-is-safe"></a>

#### 1.10.8 "If I have a job, my visa is safe."

In the UAE an employer can cancel a visa online without the worker knowing <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>. The UAE began cancelling many Bangladeshis' visas in July 2026, and on 13 October 2026 the Law Minister said 5,000 had been cancelled <sup>[N100](#n100), [N101](#n101)</sup>.

<a id="malaysia-has-opened-again"></a>

#### 1.10.9 "Malaysia has opened again."

Recruitment was announced on 15 October 2026, but on 4 November 2026 the minister said the medical tests it needs had not yet been approved <sup>[7](#o7), [N9](#n9)</sup>. BOESL ran its own call for 112 DHL jobs (see [Can I go through the government agency](#can-i-go-through-the-government-agency-boesl), BOESL?) <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>.

<a id="oman-is-opening-soon"></a>

#### 1.10.10 "Oman is opening soon."

In August 2026 the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said Oman had made no formal announcement on recruiting Bangladeshis, and in September 2026 the State Minister said visas remain restricted <sup>[N102](#n102), [N12](#n12)</sup>.

<a id="kyrgyzstan-is-a-door-to-europe"></a>

#### 1.10.11 "Kyrgyzstan is a door to Europe."

A man who paid a broker Tk 4.8 lakh on a promise of Italy was taken to Bishkek and spent 44 days in a Central Asian prison <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>.

<a id="dalal-scams-the-usual-tricks-and-how-to-spot-them"></a>

## 2 Dalal scams: the usual tricks and how to spot them

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 2.1 In one minute

Most Bangladeshis who go abroad pay a dalal (দালাল, an unlicensed broker or middleman) somewhere along the way: in a 2023 national survey, 52% of migrants paid their migration costs to brokers, and 58% borrowed to go <sup>[N104 (2024)](#n104), [N48 (2024)](#n48)</sup>. An ILO (International Labour Organization) consultant says the dalal is often a friend or relative, because people trust them with large sums more than strangers <sup>[N104 (2024)](#n104)</sup>. Behind the village dalal sit more middlemen, a licensed agency and, abroad, a sponsor or broker who sells the visa itself; one agency owner counted five to six levels of brokers <sup>[N105 (2018)](#n105), [N19](#n19)</sup>. Each scam below works by selling something that is not what it seems: a visa with no job behind it, a tourist visa called a work visa, a contract that changes on arrival, a medical or skill-test "pass" for money, a fake clearance card, a European work permit that was never issued, or a marriage that is really trafficking <sup>[N19](#n19), [N106](#n106), [N59](#n59), [63](#o63), [N107](#n107), [N108](#n108), [125](#o125)</sup>. Getting the money back is hard: in the 2026 Cambodia cases the CID (Criminal Investigation Department of the police) said many returnees were reluctant to go to law and others bargained with brokers, and complaints without documents are rejected <sup>[N107](#n107), [N109 (2019)](#n109), [N110 (2024)](#n110)</sup>. Taking money on a false job promise or holding someone's passport is a crime for anyone, not only agencies, with up to 5 years in prison, and trafficking carries at least 5 years up to life <sup>[26](#o26), [126 (2013)](#o126), [125](#o125)</sup>. Before paying, the official checks are free: BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training, the government body that licenses agencies and clears workers) checks a job visa or demand letter in 3 working days, and Saudi Arabia, Singapore and other destinations let you check a visa or permit online <sup>[10](#o10), [127](#o127), [128](#o128)</sup>.

<a id="who-a-dalal-is"></a>

### 2.2 Who a dalal is

The word dalal means broker <sup>[N111](#n111)</sup>. Since 2023 the law recognises a "sub-agent", a person registered with BMET who collects workers for a licensed agency; anyone else who does that work, or claims to, commits a crime <sup>[44 (2023)](#o44), [26](#o26)</sup>. A 2024 study of Bangladeshi migrants named recruitment through a sub-agent or dalal among the top risk factors they face <sup>[N112 (2024)](#n112)</sup>.

<a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller"></a>

#### 2.2.1 The chain from the village to the visa seller

1. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-1"></a>**The visa seller abroad.** In Saudi Arabia, a Bangladeshi agent in the trade told Bonik Barta in 2026 that sponsors (the employer or household that legally owns a worker's visa) take out work permits for workers they do not need and sell them; the worker enters as that sponsor's employee but then works for another company <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>. In Kuwait, brokers lure Kuwaiti citizens into obtaining worker visas, and a visa that costs KWD 6 (Kuwaiti dinars) in processing and attestation fees is sold for KWD 2,000-2,200 (about Tk 8-9 lakh), though the employer also spends about KWD 250 on the work permit and other costs <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. The joint general secretary of the Bangladesh Press Club in Kuwait said in July 2025 that an organised broker ring there controls the visa trade, which is why Bangladeshis pay more than other South Asians <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-2"></a>**The licensed recruiting agency in Dhaka.** It may take workers only through registered sub-agents, from BMET's database or directly <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>. In reviews from 835 Bangladeshi migrants on the ITUC's (International Trade Union Confederation) Recruitment Advisor, 78% had relied on a sub-agent to reach the agency <sup>[N113 (2023)](#n113)</sup>.
3. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-3"></a>**Layers of middlemen.** A visa passes through six or seven hands before reaching the worker, according to TIB (Transparency International Bangladesh) <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>. One agency owner said that with five to six levels of brokers, agencies cannot control the cost <sup>[N105 (2018)](#n105)</sup>.
4. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-4"></a>**The village dalal.** This is often a friend or relative, according to an ILO consultant <sup>[N104 (2024)](#n104)</sup>. In one 2025 Cambodia case the first meeting was at a tea stall across the highway from Dhaka airport; the man paid Tk 1 lakh to the dalal and the rest to a manpower agency in Fakirapool, Dhaka <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.
5. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-5"></a>**The online dalal.** Many dalals now work through Facebook pages and WhatsApp, with no signboard, no trade licence and sometimes no real name; they call themselves "recruitment consultant", "visa processing expert" or a foreign company's "official representative" <sup>[N114](#n114)</sup>.

<a id="the-law-on-sub-agents"></a>

#### 2.2.2 The law on sub-agents

- **Only registered sub-agents are legal.** Since the Overseas Employment and Migrants (Amendment) Act 2023, an agency may appoint only a registered person as its sub-agent or representative, and BMET issues the registration <sup>[130 (2023)](#o130), [129](#o129)</sup>. Under section 35 of the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013 (the OEM Act), an agency that appoints an unregistered one faces 1 year's prison or a Tk 1-5 lakh fine, and the unregistered dalal faces 1 year or a Tk 50,000 to 2 lakh fine <sup>[44 (2023)](#o44), [26](#o26)</sup>.
- **What a registered sub-agent must show.** He must run an office with a signboard giving his name, registration number and date, the agency's name and licence number, and his addresses and phone numbers, and must display his registration certificate <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>. BMET issues the certificate, and BMET or the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office, BMET's district office) publishes sub-agents' details on its website <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.
- **Where the list should be.** Each agency must publish its list of registered sub-agents on its own website, and may take workers only through registered sub-agents, from BMET's database or directly <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.
- **Who pays if he cheats.** Under the 2025 licence and sub-agent rules, the agency and its sub-agent are each and jointly liable for whatever the sub-agent does <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>. Each sub-agent lodges Tk 3 lakh security with the agency by pay order, and a worker cheated by him is compensated from it once the irregularity is proved <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>. BMET's Director General can suspend or cancel a sub-agent's registration, after an inquiry and hearing, if a complaint by a worker, a relative or a well-wisher is proved <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has BMET registered any sub-agents yet, and where exactly is the public list (which page of bmet.gov.bd or OEP)?
> - Do agencies actually publish their sub-agent lists on their websites, and how many do?

<a id="what-people-pay-and-to-whom"></a>

#### 2.2.3 What people pay, and to whom

- **The average bill.** A 2020 survey by the Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics with the ILO found a worker spends Tk 416,789 on average to go abroad <sup>[N115](#n115)</sup>. An OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation) survey put spending at five times the government-set cost on average <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
- **To whom.** In the 2023 survey, 52.03% of migrants paid their migration costs to brokers <sup>[N104 (2024)](#n104)</sup>. Broker use was highest in Sylhet (58.53%) and Mymensingh (57.44%) divisions and lowest in Barishal (28.83%) <sup>[N104 (2024)](#n104)</sup>.
- **Example of the split.** In the 2025 Cambodia case above, the dalal asked Tk 7 lakh in all for a job he said would pay Tk 80,000 a month; the man paid Tk 1 lakh to the dalal and the rest to the agency <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.
- **The official figure.** The ministry fixed the official migration cost for Saudi Arabia in 2016 at Tk 165,000 <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>. In one BMET permission letter for Saudi kitchen workers, the agency could take at most Tk 50,000 per worker <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a village dalal keep per worker on a typical Saudi or Malaysia visa in 2026, and what does the sub-agent above him keep?

<a id="fake-visa-demand-letter-or-job-offer"></a>

### 2.3 Fake visa, demand letter or job offer

A demand letter is the employer's written request for workers, attested by a Bangladesh mission abroad <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.

**How it works:**

1. A dalal or unlicensed office shows a visa, demand letter or job offer, takes the passport and money, and the worker is sent to the airport or never hears again <sup>[N117 (2020)](#n117), [N118 (2022)](#n118)</sup>.
2. Some papers are entirely forged: one Dhaka ring printed Oman visas on a computer and supplied fake tickets and fake BMET clearance <sup>[N118 (2022)](#n118)</sup>. Another office held fake Fiji demand letters and fake contracts <sup>[N117 (2020)](#n117)</sup>.
3. Some are real papers that lead nowhere: a broker told Bonik Barta in 2024 that some Saudi citizens issue visas under shell companies, and brokers disappear once paid <sup>[N119 (2024)](#n119)</sup>. A former BAIRA (Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) official said in 2026 that some Saudi visas first appear in the online system and later disappear, and industry insiders said the Saudi labour ministry had cancelled several thousand applications filed on fake demand letters <sup>[N78](#n78)</sup>.
4. An attested demand letter is not proof: TIB found some Bangladeshi mission officials attest demand letters without checks or for bribes <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- an office with no recruiting licence that advertises online and collects passports <sup>[N117 (2020)](#n117)</sup>;
- a Saudi visa whose company cannot be found in the Ministry of Commerce's commercial register (Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 1: Find a real job, Before you pay anyone) <sup>[131](#o131)</sup>.

**Check before paying:**

1. <a id="fake-visa-demand-letter-or-job-offer-item-1"></a>**At BMET or your DEMO:** take a copy of the visa or demand letter (and, where relevant, the passport page with the visa) to BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000, or your DEMO; the check is free and takes 3 working days <sup>[10](#o10), [132](#o132)</sup>. BMET checks Saudi, Qatar, Bahrain, Singapore and UAE visas online, and its visa-check app is on Google Play <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
2. <a id="fake-visa-demand-letter-or-job-offer-item-2"></a>**On the destination's own system:**
   - Saudi Arabia: KSA Visa Track, with the visa or application number and your passport number (Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 7: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>.
   - Malaysia: MyIMMs application status, with the employer's registration number and application number (Malaysia doc: Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Step 5: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>.
   - Qatar: the Interior Ministry's Visa Inquiry (Qatar doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 5: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
   - UAE: the job offer, by its reference number, on the enquiry service of MOHRE (the Ministry of Human Resources and Emiratisation), inquiry.mohre.gov.ae (UAE doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 5: Check your visa and permit are real) <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>.
   - Singapore: the "Check work pass and application status" service of MOM (the Ministry of Manpower), no login needed (Singapore doc: Work visa: Work Permit (construction, marine shipyard, process and other sectors), Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA) <sup>[128](#o128)</sup>.
   - Maldives: Work Permit Verification on xpat.egov.mv (Maldives doc: Work visa: company worker (construction, resort, shop), Step 5: Check your permit and visa are real) <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>.
   - Kyrgyzstan: Check status on the e-visa portal with the reference number (Kyrgyzstan doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 5: The work visa, and checking it is real) <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>.
   - Oman: the visa status shows only in the sponsor's own account, so only the employer can check it (Oman doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 6: The employer gets the employment visa from the ROP; check it) <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>.
   - Kuwait: BMET's check covers the visa and demand letter (Kuwait doc: Work visa: private-sector worker (Article 18), Step 5: Medical check and checking your visa is real) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- 15 Sept 2020: a RAB (Rapid Action Battalion, an elite police unit) mobile court found that "Dream Visa", Road 4, Banani, had no recruiting licence, seized 32 passports, fake contracts and fake Fiji demand letters, and jailed the owner for six months after he admitted fraud <sup>[N117 (2020)](#n117)</sup>.
- Aug 2022: RAB arrested members of an unlicensed Dhaka ring that, according to RAB, took Tk 4 lakh each and supplied fake Oman visas, fake tickets and fake BMET clearance; victims were turned back at the airport <sup>[N118 (2022)](#n118)</sup>.

**What to do:** taking money on a false job promise is an offence under section 31 of the OEM Act (see [File a police case](#file-a-police-case)) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>; complain to BMET too (see [Complain to BMET](#complain-to-bmet)) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

<a id="visa-trading-and-the-free-visa"></a>

### 2.4 Visa trading and the "free visa"

A "free visa" (ফ্রি ভিসা) is a real visa bought from a sponsor who has no job for you <sup>[N19](#n19), [N120 (2020)](#n120)</sup>.

**How it works:**

1. A sponsor abroad takes out a visa in its own name and sells it through brokers <sup>[N19](#n19), [N24](#n24)</sup>.
2. Dalals sell it in Bangladesh, though it gives only permission to enter, with no job guaranteed <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
3. On arrival in Saudi Arabia you are the sponsor's employee in name only: you must find your own work and housing, may pay the sponsor a monthly cut, and can be deported for breaking your visa's conditions <sup>[N120 (2020)](#n120)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** "free visa" (ফ্রি ভিসা), "easy job" (সহজ চাকরি), "quick departure" (কম সময়ে যাত্রা) <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>. In Malaysia the same trick is sold as a "calling visa" <sup>[N121](#n121)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- the words "free visa": BMET says there is no such thing <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>;
- a household named as the employer on a work visa, with other signs listed in Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Avoid a "free visa" <sup>[N120 (2020)](#n120)</sup>;
- a price far above the official cost: a Kuwait visa that costs KWD 6 in fees is sold for Tk 8-9 lakh <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** BMET says to have any so-called free visa and its papers checked at BMET in Dhaka or the DEMO, and to confirm the exact job, pay and benefits <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- Reported Sept 2026: hundreds of people from Munshiganj and Cumilla paid about Tk 4-6 lakh each after Facebook ads for Saudi "free visa" jobs, and many found the visa was not a work permit <sup>[N114](#n114)</sup>.
- Reported July 2024: a Pirojpur man sold his business, mortgaged land and borrowed heavily to go to Oman on a free visa through a dalal, and got no work for a year <sup>[N122 (2024)](#n122)</sup>.
- Reported Jan 2026: many who went to Malaysia on a calling visa or free visa found no work on arrival <sup>[N121](#n121)</sup>.

**What to do:** obtaining a demand letter, visa or work permit by illegal means and buying or selling it inside Bangladesh is an offence under section 33 of the OEM Act, with 2 to 7 years' rigorous imprisonment and a fine of at least Tk 3 lakh <sup>[138](#o138), [26](#o26)</sup>. The government legal aid helpline is 16699 (see [Free legal help](#free-legal-help)) <sup>[139](#o139)</sup>.

<a id="a-visit-or-tourist-visa-sold-as-a-job"></a>

### 2.5 A visit or tourist visa sold as a job

**How it works:**

1. The dalal promises a work visa but the passport carries a tourist or visit visa <sup>[N106](#n106), [N119 (2024)](#n119)</sup>.
2. The worker enters legally, the visa runs out, and the worker is illegal with no job or employer <sup>[N119 (2024)](#n119)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** that a one- or two-month tourist visa is a two-year work visa <sup>[N106](#n106)</sup>, or that a 3-month tourist visa will become a 15-month iqama (Saudi residence permit) <sup>[N119 (2024)](#n119)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- a job offered on a tourist or visit visa: the ministry warns not to go to work on one on a broker's promise <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>;
- destinations in the ministry's 5 Feb 2025 warning: the Middle East, Russia, Greece, Libya, Lebanon, Egypt, Cambodia and Malaysia <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** the visa type shows in the destination's own visa check (Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 7: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>, and BMET's free check says whether it is a work visa <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- Apr 2023 to May 2026: a Keraniganj woman, then 20, promised a Tk 50,000-a-month restaurant job in Dubai, was given a visit visa, had her passport taken on arrival and was made to work in a dance bar for three years, she told Samakal <sup>[N123](#n123)</sup>.
- Reported July 2026: Cambodia victims travelled on one- or two-month tourist visas their brokers called two-year work visas <sup>[N106](#n106)</sup>.

**What to do:** see File a police case and, if you were forced to work, File a trafficking case <sup>[26](#o26), [125](#o125)</sup>.

<a id="contract-substitution-a-different-job-or-wage-on-arrival"></a>

### 2.6 Contract substitution: a different job or wage on arrival

**How it works:**

1. The contract shown in Bangladesh names one employer, job and wage <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.
